# Shared dispatcher learnings

Started at Gary's request on 14 November 2026. Both pollers record delivery issues and their resolution here,
so improvements can be carried into agent-scaffolding. This log is shared through `gcsp-poc-dev`.

Append an event when an issue is identified, mitigated, resolved, recurs or its diagnosis changes. Use a unique
ID such as `DL-20261114-GC-001` or `DL-20261114-RG-001`, then reuse it for later events. Preserve earlier entries;
correct them with a new event. Include UTC time, poller, impact, evidence, cause/uncertainty, action, outcome and
framework follow-up. Keep raw logs, credentials, private data and personal coordination files elsewhere.

States: **identified**, **mitigating**, **verified resolved**, **reopened**. A proposed framework change remains
a proposal until implemented and checked; record its eventual commit/check evidence against the same ID.
The log does not replace required defect records, expand Stories or add a delivery gate. Pull before writing;
commit and push named paths through the existing helper, preserving concurrent appends from the other poller.

## 2026-11-14 16:51 UTC · GC Codex · DL-20261114-GC-001 · identified

- **Issue/impact:** dispatch overhead and scheduling holds reduced useful parallel delivery. At the 16:32
  observation only FUCQ-447 was confirmed active; FUCQ-442 and FUCQ-446 were held by GC scheduling.
- **Cause/evidence:** repeated separate source, test-preparation, review and runtime-release handoffs are
  recorded in FUCQ-434 coordination comments 3629457/3629458 and the Story delivery histories. The capacity
  tool at 16:50 reported `can_start=3`; resource capacity did not require keeping both independent Stories held.
- **Action/outcome:** identified as a dispatcher process problem. Existing resource ownership, original
  acceptance criteria and conflict protection remain necessary; they do not require a new permission round
  for every routine implementation/test step.
- **Framework follow-up:** make a bounded Story release include implementation and proportionate checks;
  recheck runnable existing work against actual capacity and file conflicts, rather than waiting for an
  unrelated checkpoint. Measure useful delivery before calling this resolved.

  `8cbcfe7580785dacf77cb7b8ddff598d712756ae`. App API confirmed both Emmet/FUCQ-442 and Hubie/FUCQ-447 executing.

- **Framework follow-up:** validate that the simpler release produces coherent tested integrations without
  widening the original Story or repeating preparation rounds. Framework implementation remains proposed.

## 2026-11-14 16:50 UTC · GC Codex · DL-20261114-GC-002 · mitigating

- **Issue/impact:** a global "active" count can be mistaken for complete visibility across both pollers.
- **Cause/evidence:** all 18 native In Progress Stories had leases, but nine belonged to the other poller
  without fresh execution evidence available here. Its latest observed FUCQ-434 coordination comment was
  3629458 at 10:29:52 UTC. A lease or native In Progress status cannot establish current execution or inactivity.
- **Action/outcome:** apply Gary's six exclusive buckets: To do, active, held, blocked, in review and done.
  Label those nine reservations **held — execution unverified**, with the observation time. Preserve native
  Jira statuses. Fresh native query and lease reconciliation still cover exactly the authorised 66 Stories.
- **Framework follow-up:** exchange small expiring execution observations between pollers, separate from
  leases and tracker status. Reporting semantics are applied locally; shared execution visibility is unresolved.

## 2026-11-14 16:35 UTC · GC Codex · DL-20261114-GC-003 · mitigating

- **Issue/impact:** the global Jira lifecycle planner took approximately eight minutes and produced 198
  proposals when only FUCQ-417's lifecycle needed action.
- **Cause/evidence:** the planner scanned historical adoption dates across Git refs for unrelated Stories.
  Only the authorised FUCQ-417 operation was applied; the Story is native In Review and cleaned up. Its
  acceptance disposition and evidence map are in `docs/gcsp/evidence/fucq417/`.
- **Action/outcome:** GC's local adapter now accepts an explicit issue scope before planning and rejects
  acknowledgements for foreign keys. Syntax was checked; the scoped runtime has not yet been exercised.
- **Framework follow-up:** add native scoped lifecycle planning to agent-scaffolding and verify it against
  both selected and excluded issues. Do not call the performance problem resolved from syntax checking alone.

## 2026-11-14 16:54 UTC · GC Codex · DL-20261114-GC-004 · mitigating

- **Issue/impact:** inherited output-placement and cleanup instructions conflict with Gary's explicit
  prohibition on all OneDrive access, including reads and cleanup verification.
- **Cause/evidence:** historical resource instructions require large outputs in synced storage. They cannot
  override the later human restriction. Historical observations retained locally are not fresh cloud audits.
- **Action/outcome:** current worker releases prohibit all such access and use bounded local scratch with
  the resource helper's explicit local reason and `/private/tmp` output root. Persisted the restriction in
  shared `AGENTS.md` and linked it from `CLAUDE.md`; no OneDrive inspection or cleanup was performed for this log.
- **Framework follow-up:** make explicit forbidden storage locations binding throughout allocation, imports,
  finalizers and audits; support local output policy without a cloud-aware runner. Do not verify this by
  probing a forbidden location. Future checks must retain resource limits and report any resulting limitation.

## 2026-11-16 19:30 UTC · Russell Claude Code · DL-20261116-RG-003 · scratch-schema query plans make the Java buddy replay slow

- **Evidence:** FUCQ-507's full-size buddy replay (seed comparison-b) processed only 5 to 6 contenders a second, about 80 to 90 minutes for the first release alone. `Claim.alreadyTaken` took 154 ms per call, 97% of DB time.
- **Cause:** pgjdbc server-prepares a statement after 5 uses. Its generic plan was cached while the fresh scratch schema's tables were empty, and they had no statistics, so each call scanned every contender.
- **Action (applied, verified):** the comparison-only `ReplayConnection` now sets `prepareThreshold=0`, uses async commit, and runs `ANALYZE allocation_attempts` every 500 commits. The call now takes 0.47 ms. Buddy took 913 s, against 2,070 s before the fix, and its records match fresh Node on every field except `codeRevision`. Allocation code is unchanged (`fa5f87d58` on `dev/DEV-2026-11-16-026`).
- **Proposed improvement:** any Java path that seeds a fresh schema and then loops over it should refresh its statistics, or avoid generic cached plans. Measure one full-size run early, before building further slices.

## 2026-11-16 23:47 UTC - GC Claude - DL-20261116-GCC-006 - 07 and 08 ran as two concurrent implementers

- **What happened:**
  - 08 arrived at 23:35:28Z. The watcher, re-armed straight after spawning 07, woke at 23:35:58Z, and I acknowledged 08 at 23:36:25Z. That is a 57 s pickup, against more than an hour for 07.

  - Both returned source with tests AUTHORED/UNRUN. I reviewed each against Node and checkpointed them separately: cec4d3c2b and bd8204652.
- **What worked:**
  - Re-arm the watcher in the background right after a background spawn.
  - Give each implementer the exact absolute paths, a "do not touch" list naming the other worker's files, and "write early".
  - Checkpoint each worker's files with an explicit pathspec, so neither commit picks up the other's or foreign untracked files.
- **Shell pitfall (parent):** zsh does not word-split `$F`. `git add -- $F` with a space-separated string becomes one path and fails. Use an array, `F=(a b c)`.
- **Review learnings from the children:**
  - A stamp-reading adapter and its clock must share one time base. Pin it with an `asOf == clock.now` check. Safe here, because the run clock is a stored instant that only moves on ticks and bumps its version when it does.
  - Node planning `validInstant` and the vehicles strict `date` differ. An offset instant passes the read and fails the terms build, so test both.
  - A `PolicyService` subclass with `super(null)` is a cheap real-service double for pin checks.
  - `find /` hung a child's shell. Children should use only the paths the brief names.

## 2026-11-17 00:59 UTC - GC Claude - DL-20261117-GCC-007 - 09 stopped at its precondition; a clock fix moved the failure

- **What happened:**
  - Codex pre-authorised two finite chunks for 09, conditional on an independent review finding no blocker or major issue.
  - Toothless (reviewer) found M1, which I verified. Manual cart holds get `expires_at` from ClaimClock, then PolicyClock, then WallPolicyClock (wall time). bd8204652 moved the planner onto the run clock, so after a presenter jump ahead every plan read is a 503 `planning-held-stock-unresolved`.
  - I started no runtime and returned three options. The conditional pre-authorisation did its job: the gate was decided from evidence, with no extra round trip.
- **Root-cause learning:** switching a reader's clock affects every timestamp column it compares against. List each column and the clock that writes it before choosing the fix. Review D1 looked at the release stamps only, so the fix moved the failure onto hold stamps.
- **Coordination learnings:**
  - Long reviews at about 250k context still hadn't written their file after an hour, despite "write first" in the brief. Check the agent's file and transcript at about the 45-minute mark, and ask it to bank its findings.

## 2026-11-17 04:06 UTC - GC Claude - DL-20261117-GCC-009 - 12: a splice slip, a propagation miss, and a cross-writer gap the review caught

- **The supervisor draft had a syntax error, and the parent caught it, not me.**
  - I built the demand12 selection by splicing a generated `ALL = {...}` block into a copy of 09. I kept 09's closing `}` as well, which left an extra brace at line 72.
  - My check counted test names, not structure. Source-only work bars an interpreter, so I never parsed it. Codex's `ast.parse` found it at 03:44.
  - Fixed at 03:47 by deleting that one line, after asserting lines 71 and 72 were both a bare `}`. I then counted brackets by text.
  - **Improvement:** when no interpreter is allowed, count bracket balance on every spliced region before publishing, and cross-check the names against the `@Test` methods. Both take one line of `tr` and `awk`.
- **Propagation miss, on my side.**
  - The second-batch briefs (Mushu and Mike Wazowski) named the files, the contract and the OneDrive and runtime bans. They left out the full mandatory reference list: AGENTS.md, build-rules, experience-quality, readiness, learnings and the original AC. They also left out the rule that descendants must pass these on.
  - The cause: I wrote them from my compacted state, not from a fixed envelope.
  - The 03:35 relay reached the reviewer, who was still running. Mushu had already finished, so the relay was too late for them.
  - My source handover also proposed raw `git add`/`commit`. The commit actually went through `scaffold_git.py commit --paths <7>`, dry-run first, as `76837ffe8`.
  - **Improvement:** keep one envelope file per assignment (references, allocation, bans, the descendant rule) and paste it verbatim into every child brief. A relay cannot reach a child that has already finished.
- **The independent review found a gap my interface freeze missed.**
  - I took the hold shape from the Buddy writer (`Claim.java`), which stores `attributes.renewalId`. The manual-claim writer (`ManualClaims.java`) stores none.
  - So a manually allocated employee was never `already-selected` and would have been counted again as demand. That breaks AC1.
  - Fixed as D7: the reader falls back to the slot's renewal, or null. A regression test runs the snapshot through the cohort.
  - **Improvement:** when freezing a snapshot interface, list every writer of each table across packages, not just the one nearest the feature.
- **Coverage limit, accepted and not chased:** Codex's 04:00 point stands. The explicit-null hold sits on a slot with no renewal, so that one assertion can't tell "kept null" from "fell back to null". The code branch is correct, and the limit is recorded rather than turned into a new campaign.
- **How this was published:** the helper's `push` covers only the base branch, so this entry went out as the earlier ones did. That meant a temporary detached worktree on `origin/gcsp-poc-dev`, a commit of this file only and a fast-forward push, with the worktree removed afterwards. No OneDrive access.

## 2026-11-17 14:45 UTC · Russell Claude Code · DL-20261116-RG-002 · F507 merged; hosted dev sizing and a shared policy value changed

- **For Gary's poller (hosting owner):** FUCQ-507 merged to `gcsp-poc-dev` at `d3b5497cc`. A Greg-started full comparison job (three seeds, 1,000 employees) on the started Java app took 4,118 s and matched a fresh Node run exactly on all 6 per-seed record digests.
- **Hosted dev sizing:** a full Java comparison job needs about a 3 GB heap. Under `-Xmx1536m` it ran out of memory after 180 s and was recorded honestly as failed; the live set after a full GC is about 1.2 to 1.5 GB, and the measured peak RSS is 3.0 to 3.1 GB. With the Dockerfile's `MaxRAMPercentage=75`, that means a Render instance of about 4 GB if hosted dev is to run comparison jobs. `render-service.proposed.yaml` names no plan; the instance size is the hosting owner's choice.
- **Shared policy value changed (your lane edits this file too):** `server/gcsp/policy/seed-v1.json` `comparison.jobs.v1.timeoutSeconds` went from 1800 to 10800, with its basis updated. The Java buddy route takes 913 to 1,918 s per seed, so 1,800 s could never finish. The parity fixtures were regenerated by their Node generators. The value isn't in the comparison policyHash, so stored records don't change. Expect a merge touchpoint if your branch edits nearby rows.
- **Also (from F458, a14cd1b88):** hosted dev still needs `GCSP_ACCOUNT_PROVIDER=repository` and `GCSP_SCENARIO_PROVIDER=demo`.
- **Linked-Bug candidate:** several `*HttpProfiles` hard-code the database name `gcsp_test_dev`, so they fail on a shared cluster with per-story database names (`PersistenceProfile`, `BriefHttpProfiles` and others).
- **Limitation:** all of this is local only (an M-series Mac under heavy load, PG17). No hosted run exists.

## 2026-11-17 18:00 UTC · Russell Claude Code · DL-20261117-RG-002 · Memory exhaustion crash

- **What happened:** the Mac reached 100% memory and swap and locked up, and had to be restarted. Russell's poller had three story-leads, each with an implementer and a Quarkus Maven JVM. The dispatcher had started the third lead as a lane replacement while the capacity check said `can_start=0` (10.6 GB of swap).
- **Resolution:** branches pushed, the shared PG cluster recreated, and leads resumed one at a time. A lane replacement is a new start: it needs `can_start>0` like any other. `/tmp` scratch (PG clusters, story DBs) does not survive a reboot.
